      $ .venv/bin/python - <<'PY'
    from pathlib import Path
    from html.parser import HTMLParser
    from zipfile import ZipFile
    root = Path('.sources/kadri-2024-hydroacoustics/outputs/kadri-meeting-visual-pack')
    class Links(HTMLParser):
        def __init__(self):
            super().__init__()
            self.targets = []
        def handle_starttag(self, tag, attrs):
            for key, value in attrs:
                if key in ('src', 'href') and value and not value.startswith(('http:', 'https:', '#', 'data:')):
                    self.targets.append(value.split('#')[0])
    for name in ('index.html', 'graphics-gallery.html'):
        parser = Links()
        parser.feed((root / name).read_text())
        missing = [x for x in parser.targets if not (root / x).is_file()]
        assert not missing, missing
        print(f'{name}: {len(parser.targets)} local links checked')
    with ZipFile(root / 'kadri-meeting-visual-pack.zip') as archive:
        assert archive.testzip() is None
        for stem in ('12-pleiades-conditional-summary', '08a-oscar-source-time-alignment', '09-airgun-nuisance-source-model', '03b-impact-state-to-pressure'):
            for suffix in ('png', 'svg'):
                name = f'assets/{stem}.{suffix}'
                assert archive.read(name) == (root / name).read_bytes(), name
        print('Download archive: CRC check passed; all revised PNG/SVG files match')

This is the presentation I went through with Dr Kadri yesterday. It went well and he's open to collaboration. He suggested two options, one is to check things which don'e take too much time, the other is to engage in a bigger project which would involve a post doc for up to a year. That costs 40-50k GBP, I could fund maybe 25% of that, we'd need to find other sponsors, let me know if you have thoughts on that. Also, does the University of Cardiff have any programmes for public-private partnership, for example if I funded part of a post doc through a business are there any routes to matching or getting some contribution from the university? also what about research councils, grants? or aviation safety foundations or sources of funding related to MH370. Also, what if we formed a non profit and donated from that, any benefits? (including UK/US tax for me).  In general, I want to send this deck to him but include some next steps. I think of it as Option A and B on the slide "how can acoustics enter the Bayesian estimator'. Option A I try to get him to help us test some conditional hypotheses, for example the Pleiades conditional hypothesis, the conditional hypothesis of an unarrested Holland descent, and the conditional hypotheses inherent in the table we sent. For his preferred candidate and also others in the table, I think it would be good if we try some of the DG filtering/beamforming so we can test that relatively short list from two locations. Then the larger project with postdoc etc would be Option B where we integrate the hydroacoustic sources into the Bayesian estimator. I don't anticipate that this would yield a single solution, but under a set of assumptions which would be explicit, this observable I anticipate would modify the PDF, de-weighting some impact trajectories (e.g. those with high energy which should be observed but aren't) while perhaps adding more weight to others (for example where there's a corresponding signal at CL/DG or both which would match the trajectory arrival time and energy coupling range), so we get a PDF using satcom, ocean drift, and acoustics. Make sense? could you help me formulate additional slides which propose the next steps. We should also be explicit about the events we want to test in the first step, which includes the items in the table (and also I have a slide with CL 00:38 and 00:55 arrival time events which I am note sure are the same as those in the table, help me reconcile that please). We need to prioritize these items. Regarding publication, he's not in a harry to publish and doesn't like it much when it's a hurry. But on the other hand I feel some urgency to get something out there before Ocean Infinity goes back out. I think the compromise suggestion is: 1/ get the JON paper drafted and pre-pub which includes the broader summary of work and includes some of the initial conditional hypotheses results for hydroacoustic, and heavily points toward further research. This gets something out there but also could be helpful when trying to get sponsors to fund further work. Then we follow later with a more extensive paper or series of papers. Thoughts?

      following the recommendations by the official investigation the signal of interest that is associated with the 
      impact has to occur around the 7th arc, both in time and location. Around 00:30 UTC there are a few repeating 
      signals (Fig. 9a) followed by no observed signals (Fig. 9b), around the requested time window. After that, there 
      are only two signals that were observed within the requested time window, as shown in Fig. 9c. The first signal 
      has a bearing of 57◦ and thus cannot be related to MH370, but the second, which was recorded at 00:52 UTC 
      at H01W, arrives from a plausible direction, i.e., 306◦ . Notably, the transect from the 7th arc to H01W has no 
      bathymetric irregularities (see Fig. 15c in “Methods”), and thus minimum loss of energy is expected due to scat-
      tering. Confirming the same signal of interest at H08S would be a challenging task if at all possible due to the 
      very high noise distortion (most likely airgun), and the significant bathymetric rise midway along the transect, 
      that is expected to significantly reduce the transmission of the signal. The expected pressure amplitude at H08S,

      Figure 14 shows the possible combinations of distance and time that would have been travelled. Note that both 
      ¯r and ¯v are vectors defined positive in the bearing direction, i.e., inwards the 7th arc. Illustration of the possible 
      time duration and distance travelled (Eq. 4) as a function of the aircraft average velocity (defined positive in the 
      direction of the bearing, travelling away). Hence, for this event to be associated with MH370, the aircraft must 
      have remained in air for at least 10 min prior to impacting the water surface. Even for a considerably low aver-
      age velocity of 1450 m/s, it would require the aircraft to remain in air about 13 min after the last handshake, and 
      travel an extra distance of about 350 km. Finally, adding a tolerance of 0.5◦ in the bearing calculations provides 
      the possible impact scenario depicted in Fig. 10. Later impact (blue area) is associated with lower average veloc-
      ity travelled in opposite direction from H01W, and thus closer distance to the 7th arc, whereas Earlier impact is 
      associated with spending a longer time in air with a higher average velocity resulting in travelling further away 
      from the 7th arc, opposite to H01W. It is possible that the aircraft crash is outwards the 7th arc in the direction 
      of H01W, but that will require the aircraft to last a much longer time in the air (above 17 min), before finally 
      impacting the water.
      It is worth noting that the signal with bearing 268.24◦ recorded at 00:39:02 UTC is the only signal with t = 0 , 
      thus if originated at the exact 7th arc time, it would also be exact location. However, this signal is faint and would 
      require further analysis, perhaps alongside the suggested field experiment.

       Small grants programme 2026 | Lloyd's Register Foundation Skip to main content Please enable JavaScript in your web browser to get the best experience. Lloyd's Register Foundation Menu Our priorities Go back Our priorities Safer maritime systems Safer sustainable infrastructure Skilled people for safer engineering Global Safety Evidence Centre Heritage Centre Regions Go back Regions Africa Americas Asia Oceania Europe Middle East World Risk Poll Go back World Risk Poll About the Poll Go back About the Poll Understanding the Poll Themes and Reports Go back Themes and Reports 2026: Climate Change 2026: Risk Perception 2024: Resilience 2024: Waste 2024: Occupational Safety 2024: Risk Perception 2021: Resilience 2021: Risk Perception 2021: Data and AI 2021: Violence and Harassment 2021 World Risk Poll 2019 World Risk Poll Data Go back Data Country Overviews Risk Indexes Resilience Index Contact the World Risk Poll team Partnerships and funding Go back Partnerships and funding Partnerships Programmes Grant funding NEST News Go back News Events Publications About us Go back About us Our strategy Our people Diversity and inclusion Our history Our purpose Search Close Site search Please enter a search term Submit search query Search You are here: News Small grants programme 2026 Applications for Lloyd's Register Foundation's small grants programme will open from 19 October to 14 December 2026. This page is approximately a 4 minute read This page was published on 01/02/2025 Lloyd's Register Foundation small grants programme - for grants up to £10,000 GBP. Apply here Watch our webinar to find out more Explore our full list of FAQs Overview This form is for completing your application for a grant up to the value above. The ethos of the programme is that "a little can go a long way". Grants of up to £10,000 for projects of up to 12 months will be available to support activities that: Initiate, build, or consolidate partnerships with organisations in the relevant sectors. Test out ideas through pilot studies, proof of concept projects or activities targeting the heritage, maritime and public sectors. World Risk Poll: Utilising insights from any World Risk Poll wave to date, including secondary analysis, evidence generation, synthesis, or exploration of a specific issue, with the aim of producing insights, learning, or resources that are relevant and useful to and demonstrate clear value for a specific audience or user group. We will only consider applications related to the charitable mission of Lloyd’s Register Foundation. Projects or activities should demonstrate the potential to lead to future collaborations, clear knowledge exchange, or create/accelerate impact. Please note grants are not normally awarded consecutively and can be no more than 12 months long. We will not consider applications for: Work which is not within the Foundation's mission For individuals, where activities only directly benefit the applicant Conservation of historic vessels Projects focused solely on naval history Capital works School, college or university fees Retrospective funding Lobbying and campaigning Business / first-class travel Core equipment, however specialist equipment would be considered on a case by case basis. Funding for a component of an already-funded project (such as journal publication costs, additional travel, sub-projects) Subscribe to the Foundation's newsletter and follow us on Linkedin and BlueSky to keep up to date with our small grants funding rounds. Watch now Small grants funding opportunity – FAQ’s 1. Who is eligible to apply – can individuals, universities, or charities apply? Open Our small grants programme is open to non-profit organisations, as well as profit organisations delivering work on a non-profit basis. Eligible applicants include registered charities, universities, and verifiable individual entities working in fields aligned with our charitable mission. Applicants must be able to responsibly manage funds, deliver measurable outcomes, and provide recent financial documentation (e.g. annual accounts). 2. Can applications come from outside the UK, and can projects be delivered internationally? Open Yes. We fund work globally, including locally led initiatives, provided the organisation meets our eligibility criteria and the project delivers demonstrable public benefit aligned with our mission. 3. Can more than one project from the same organisation be funded within the same call? Open Generally, only one application per lead organisation will be considered per call to ensure fairness. However, additional applications with distinct leads and delivery teams may be allowed. Any organisation worldwide can apply - most funding is expected to go to organisations outside the UK, particularly in developing economies. 4. Is partnership or collaboration required? Open Collaboration is not mandatory, but partnerships are encouraged - especially where they bring technical expertise, local engagement, or extend the reach of learning. 5. Would you consider co-funding if a project already has partial support from another source? Open Yes. Co-funding is acceptable if the overall project aligns with our mission and the Foundation’s contribution is distinct and non-duplicative. 6. What types of projects are within the scope of the small grants scheme? Open Projects must advance the Foundation’s mission to enhance the safety of life and property, particularly through engineering, risk, or systems approaches. We welcome both technical and non-technical projects that demonstrate clear public benefit. 7. Do you fund salaries? Open The Foundation does not fund full salaries or general overheads. However, reasonable staff time dedicated to project delivery (e.g. coordination, convening) can be included if proportionate and clearly justified. 8. How do you define “public benefit”? Open Projects must contribute to improved safety or reduced risk for people and communities - not solely benefit private or commercial interests. Findings should be shareable and publicly accessible. 9. What are the key assessment criteria? Open We assess proposals against four criteria: Alignment with the Foundation’s mission and strategy Feasibility and proportionality Innovation and learning potential Delivery and accountability 10. What is the maximum and minimum amount that can be requested? Open Applicants may request up to £10,000. Smaller requests are welcome where proportionate to the scope and outcomes. 11. How and where do applicants submit proposals? Open Applications are submitted through the Foundation’s online grants portal, accessible via the website.       #!/usr/bin/env python3
      """Reproducible follow-up proposal; no new acoustic inference or estimator updates.

      Inputs: the supplied 11 September 2026 deck, Kadri Table 1, and accompanying

      from matplotlib.backends.backend_pdf import PdfPages
      from markdown_it import MarkdownIt

      # Values transcribed from the supplied deck, pp. 4 and 9, not recalculated here.

      def canvas(title, subtitle, footer):
          fig = plt.figure(figsize=(16, 9), dpi=120, facecolor="white")
          ax = fig.add_axes([0, 0, 1, 1], xlim=(0, 16), ylim=(0, 9))
          ax.axis("off")
          put(ax, .6, 8.53, title, 14.8, .45, size=27, weight="bold")
          put(ax, .62, 7.97, subtitle, 14.75, .40, size=14, colour=MUTED)
          ax.plot([.6, 15.4], [7.42, 7.42], c=PURPLE, lw=2)
          ax.plot([.6, 15.4], [.58, .58], c=BORDER, lw=1)
          put(ax, .62, .40, footer, 14.7, .27, size=10, colour=MUTED)
          fig._briefing_text = [] if not hasattr(fig, "_briefing_text") else fig._briefing_text
          return fig, ax


      def put(ax, x, top, message, width, height, *, size=16, weight="normal", colour=INK):
          """Fit using actual glyph extents, preserving explicit paragraph breaks."""
          fig = ax.figure
          if not hasattr(fig, "_briefing_text"):
              fig._briefing_text = []
          renderer = fig.canvas.get_renderer()
          from matplotlib.font_manager import FontProperties
          font = FontProperties(family="DejaVu Sans", size=size, weight=weight)
          max_pixels = width / 16 * fig.bbox.width
          lines = []
          for para in message.split("\n"):
              current = ""
              for word in para.split():
                  trial = (current + " " + word).strip()
                  if current and renderer.get_text_width_height_descent(trial, font, False)[0] > max_pixels:
                      lines.append(current)
                      current = word
                  else:
                      current = trial
              lines.append(current)
          artist = ax.text(x, top, "\n".join(lines), va="top", ha="left", fontsize=size,
                           fontweight=weight, color=colour, linespacing=1.3)
          fig._briefing_text.append((artist, (x, top-height, width, height)))
          return artist


      def box(ax, x, bottom, width, height, title, body, *, colour=PURPLE, size=16):
          ax.add_patch(FancyBboxPatch((x, bottom), width, height,
                       boxstyle="round,pad=0.01,rounding_size=0.12", fc=PALE, ec=BORDER, lw=1))
          put(ax, x+.25, bottom+height-.24, title, width-.5, .62, size=20, weight="bold", colour=colour)
          put(ax, x+.25, bottom+height-1.02, body, width-.5, height-1.22, size=size)


      def table(ax, columns, rows, x=.65, top=6.96, heights=None, widths=None, size=14):
          widths = widths or [14.7 / len(columns)] * len(columns)
          heights = heights or [1.07] * len(rows)
          left = np.r_[x, x+np.cumsum(widths)]
          header_height=.69
          ax.add_patch(FancyBboxPatch((x, top-header_height), sum(widths), header_height,
                                    boxstyle="square,pad=0", fc=PURPLE, ec="none"))
          for i, label in enumerate(columns):
              put(ax, left[i]+.12, top-.12, label, widths[i]-.24, header_height-.18,
                  size=size, weight="bold", colour="white")
          y=top-header_height
          for row_idx, (row, height) in enumerate(zip(rows, heights)):
              ax.add_patch(FancyBboxPatch((x, y-height), sum(widths), height,
                            boxstyle="square,pad=0", fc=PALE if row_idx % 2 == 0 else "white", ec="none"))
              for i, value in enumerate(row):
                  put(ax, left[i]+.12, y-.17, value, widths[i]-.24, height-.27,
                      size=size, weight="bold" if i == 0 else "normal")
              ax.plot([x, left[-1]], [y-height, y-height], c=BORDER, lw=.8)
              y -= height


      def save(fig, stem, title, note):
          fig.canvas.draw()
          renderer = fig.canvas.get_renderer()
          texts=[]
          for artist, (x, y, w, h) in fig._briefing_text:
              b=artist.get_window_extent(renderer)
              xy=fig.axes[0].transData.transform([[x,y],[x+w,y+h]])
              tolerance=2.0
              if b.x0 < xy[0,0]-tolerance or b.x1 > xy[1,0]+tolerance or b.y0 < xy[0,1]-tolerance or b.y1 > xy[1,1]+tolerance:
                  raise AssertionError(f"Text escapes allocation in {stem}: {artist.get_text()!r}")
              texts.append(b)
          # All prose blocks have disjoint allocations; assert rendered blocks do too.
          for i,a in enumerate(texts):
              for b in texts[i+1:]:
                  overlap_x=min(a.x1,b.x1)-max(a.x0,b.x0)
                  overlap_y=min(a.y1,b.y1)-max(a.y0,b.y0)
                  if overlap_x>2 and overlap_y>2:
                      raise AssertionError(f"Overlapping prose blocks in {stem}")
          fig.savefig(OUT/f"{stem}.png", dpi=180)
          fig.savefig(OUT/f"{stem}.svg", metadata={"Date":"2026-09-12"})
          CHECKS.append({"slide":stem, "text_blocks":len(texts), "allocation_and_overlap_check":"pass"})
          FIGURES.append((fig,stem,title,note))

          ax.text(.95,5.80,r"$w_i^{\mathrm{new}}\ \propto\ w_i\int p(y_{\mathrm{CL}},y_{\mathrm{DG}}\mid z_i,\theta,M)\,p(\theta\mid z_i,M)\,\mathrm{d}\theta$",fontsize=25,color=PURPLE,va="top")

          a.set(xlim=(0,1),ylim=(0,1),xlabel="Calibrated detection probability, $P_D$",ylabel="No-target-detection factor, $1-P_D$")

          a.scatter([.1,.9],[.9,.1],s=75,color=PURPLE,zorder=3)
          a.tick_params(labelsize=12);a.xaxis.label.set_fontsize(13);a.yaxis.label.set_fontsize(13)
          put(ax,8.0,4.25,"A missing signal can de-weight a trajectory…",7.05,.75,size=20,weight="bold",colour=RED)
          put(ax,8,3.22,"…if that trajectory should have been detectable under the stated model.\n\nIf coupling, path loss or interference makes detection unlikely, absence changes little.\n\nA compatible transient helps only relative to an explicit background-event model.",7.05,2.35,size=17)
          save(fig,"bayesian-integration","Integrated likelihood and non-detection","Here z is impact location, UTC, mass, velocity and geometry; theta contains source, path, response and nuisance parameters. The curve is the single-station target-detection term, conditional on a specified detector and valid coverage. A full no-trigger likelihood also includes false alarms.       CSS="""body{font:17px/1.6 system-ui,sans-serif;color:#182638;background:#fafbfe;margin:0}main{max-width:1200px;margin:auto;padding:32px}h1,h2,h3{color:#603986;line-height:1.25}h2{margin-top:2em}img{width:100%;background:white;border:1px solid #d8dde5;border-radius:8px}a{color:#603986}nav{padding:16px;background:#efedf5;border-radius:8px}table{border-collapse:collapse;width:100%;font-size:15px;display:block;overflow:auto}th,td{border:1px solid #d8dde5;padding:10px;vertical-align:top}th{background:#efedf5}pre{overflow:auto;background:#efedf5;padding:16px}blockquote{border-left:4px solid #603986;padding-left:20px}section{margin:36px 0}p{max-width:1050px}.label{color:#526275;font-size:14px}@media print{nav{display:none}section{break-inside:avoid}body{background:white}main{padding:0}}"""


      def page(title, content):
          return f'<!doctype html><html lang="en-GB"><meta charset="utf-8"><meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width,initial-scale=1"><title>{html.escape(title)}</title><style>{CSS}</style><body><main>{content}</main></body></html>'


      def main():
          OUT.mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True)
          build_slides()
          renderer=MarkdownIt("commonmark").enable("table")
          notes=(SOURCE/"kadri-collaboration-proposal.md").read_text()
          funding=(SOURCE/"kadri-funding-and-giving.md").read_text()
          gallery='<h1>Kadri collaboration: proposed next steps</h1><p class="label">Draft for discussion · 12 September 2026 · nine additional presentation graphics</p><nav><a href="collaboration-briefing.html">Full notes and event reconciliation</a> · <a href="collaboration-slides.pdf">Slides PDF</a> · <a href="kadri-collaboration-shareable.zip">Download shareable pack</a></nav>'
          for _,stem,title,note in FIGURES:
              gallery += f'<section id="{stem}"><h2>{html.escape(title)}</h2><a href="{stem}.png"><img src="{stem}.png" alt="{html.escape(title)}"></a><p><a href="{stem}.png">PNG</a> · <a href="{stem}.svg">SVG</a></p><p>{html.escape(note)}</p></section>'
          (OUT/"graphics.html").write_text(page("Collaboration graphics",gallery))
          (OUT/"collaboration-briefing.html").write_text(page("Kadri collaboration proposal",'<nav><a href="graphics.html">View all graphics</a> · <a href="collaboration-proposal.md">Download text</a></nav>'+renderer.render(notes)+gallery))
          (OUT/"funding-and-giving.html").write_text(page("Private funding and giving notes",renderer.render(funding)))
          (OUT/"collaboration-proposal.md").write_text(notes)
          (OUT/"funding-and-giving.md").write_text(funding)
          with (OUT/"event-register.csv").open("w") as f:
              w=csv.DictWriter(f,fieldnames=list(EVENTS[0]));w.writeheader();w.writerows(EVENTS)
          with PdfPages(OUT/"collaboration-slides.pdf",metadata={"Title":"Proposed Kadri collaboration", "CreationDate":None,"ModDate":None}) as pdf:
              for fig,*_ in FIGURES:pdf.savefig(fig);plt.close(fig)
          (OUT/"layout-checks.json").write_text(json.dumps(CHECKS,indent=2)+"\n")
          inputs=[Path(__file__),DECK,SOURCE/"kadri-collaboration-proposal.md",SOURCE/"kadri-funding-and-giving.md",SOURCE/"data/kadri-table1-transients.csv",SOURCE/"paper/paper.pdf"]
          manifest={"created_for":"2026-09-12", "scientific_status":"proposal; no new acoustic likelihood or posterior", "stochastic_run":False,
                    "inputs":[{"path":str(p),"sha256":hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest()} for p in inputs],"layout_checks":CHECKS}
          (OUT/"manifest.json").write_text(json.dumps(manifest,indent=2)+"\n")
          # The shareable archive deliberately excludes personal tax notes and donor details.
          public=[OUT/"graphics.html",OUT/"collaboration-briefing.html",OUT/"collaboration-proposal.md",OUT/"collaboration-slides.pdf",OUT/"event-register.csv",OUT/"layout-checks.json"]
          public += [OUT/f"{stem}.{ext}" for _,stem,_,_ in FIGURES for ext in ["png","svg"]]
          with zipfile.ZipFile(OUT/"kadri-collaboration-shareable.zip","w",zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED) as z:
              for p in public:
                  info=zipfile.ZipInfo(p.name,(2026,9,12,0,0,0));info.compress_type=zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED
                  z.writestr(info,p.read_bytes())
          print(json.dumps({"output":str(OUT),"slides":len(FIGURES),"layout_checks":"all passed"},indent=2))


      if __name__=="__main__":main()

      # Proposed collaboration with Dr Usama Kadri

      Follow-up to the 11 September 2026 meeting. Prepared 12 September 2026. **Draft for discussion, not an agreed programme or a report of new detections.**

      ## Recommendation

      Proceed with a bounded Option A while developing the scope and funding for Option B. The distinction is scientific, not simply the size of the computing run:

      - **Option A — conditional hypotheses:** do specified features or non-detections agree with a named source/flight hypothesis under stated assumptions?
      - **Option B — integrated evidence:** can a calibrated statistical model of the station records change trajectory weights, including accounting for missed detections and unrelated events?

      The expected outcome of Option B is an updated distribution, not necessarily one location. It may shift, broaden, split or barely change the distribution. Failure to resolve coupling or interference sufficiently is also an informative outcome; it must not be disguised by a narrow assumed prior.

      The nine accompanying graphics cover the options, event reconciliation, priorities, pair controls, pilot workflow, Bayesian integration, research programme, funding and publication. PNGs are ready to insert in slides; SVGs retain vector text. The shareable download does not contain the separate personal tax-planning notes.

      ## Reconciliation of the presentation

      ### “CL 00:38 and 00:55 UTC Arrival Time Events” — presentation page 10

      The slide shows a histogram over **all 19 entries in Kadri’s Table 1**, spanning **00:38:29–00:55:07 UTC**. It is not a demonstration of two additional detections at rounded times 00:38 and 00:55. Its dominant selected entries, **00:49:58 and 00:53:31**, are already rows A and B of the page 9 table. The literal endpoints are separate catalogue entries, 00:38:29 at 261.24° and 00:55:07 at 314.15°, but the heading does not distinguish those from the full interval.

      Suggested replacement title: **“Kadri Table 1: conditional compatibility of arrivals, 00:38:29–00:55:07 UTC”.** The published bearings and times come from [Kadri (2024), Table 1, p. 14](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-024-60529-1); the histogram weights are our conditional model calculations, not measured association probabilities.

      Keep the broader CL 00:38–01:15 and DG 01:00–01:35 intervals on presentation page 11 separate: these were proposed **reception coverage windows**, not observed events or universal 95%/99% confidence intervals. Recalculate the actual acquisition bounds from the full agreed source/impact-time domain, with margins for preprocessing and control data. Those rounded windows should not truncate the raw-data request silently.

      ### Two important additional clarifications

      1. **The paper has a timing inconsistency.** Page 9 prose assigns approximately 00:52 to the 306° candidate, whereas Fig. 9’s two rectangles and the Table 1 caption distinguish ~00:52/57° from 00:54:30/306.18°. Use the latter as the working catalogue identity and ask Kadri to confirm onset, peak, rounding and channel conventions. Do not describe these as two possible times for one signal without that clarification. [Kadri (2024), pp. 9 and 14](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-024-60529-1).
      2. **The deck mixes two preferred-candidate geometries.** Page 4’s newer overlay uses ~25.85°S, 1,577 km and impact 00:36:08–00:37:46; page 9’s older table uses 25.79°S, 1,586 km and 00:36:01–00:37:40. These are different geometric conventions, not different acoustic events. This proposal uses page 4 for that candidate. The other event envelopes remain labelled as inherited page 9 results; they have not been regenerated against a new posterior here.

      ## Proposed event priorities

      All station arrivals and impact times below are on **8 March 2014, UTC**. Bearings are source-reported azimuths from CL. Impact windows and coordinates are **conditional calculations inherited from the supplied deck**, not independently measured ranges or 95% intervals. They use the stated 1.43–1.57 km s⁻¹ celerity sensitivity. Do not attach a current PDF percentile without rerunning the matching archived posterior and geometry. Work priority reflects information and effort, not probability of aircraft association.

      | Priority / question | CL arrival; reported bearing | DG arrival | Conditional impact UTC / source | Present status and next test |
      | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
      | First pass: preferred candidate | 00:54:30; 306.18° | Not identified | 00:36:08–00:37:46; arc ~25.85°S | Author-marked. Confirm timestamp and raw-array bearing uncertainty; predict a DG search from that source family. |
      | First pass: catalogue A | 00:49:58; 260.41° | Not identified | 00:26:22–00:28:29; ~35.88°S, 91.79°E | No distinct peak recovered from publication trace. Establish exactly what raw feature generated the table entry. |
      | First pass: catalogue B | 00:53:31; 257.58° | Not identified | 00:28:38–00:30:52; ~36.74°S, 90.52°E | Same raw-data verification; do not promote spatial fit to detection evidence. |
      | First pass: rectangle 1 / bearing control | ~00:52:00; 57°; digitised energy peak 00:52:04.350 | Not identified | Unresolved under the reported bearing | Prominent CL feature, but 57° does not support the southern-source interpretation. Check the triad before developing a paired-source claim. |
      | Early-impact control | 00:39:02; 268.24° | Not identified | Not re-derived here | Kadri calls this faint and highlights its timing on p. 14. Useful for the early-impact conditional; not the same pick as 00:39:23.700. |
      | Second pass: OSCAR-conditioned timing pair | 00:52:04.350 | 01:10:00.500 | 00:28:38–00:30:44; ~34.92°S, 92.05°E | Same CL feature as rectangle 1, not a new CL event. DG is at a cropped periodic-pulse boundary. Test using uninterrupted triads. |
      | Second pass: late timing pair | 00:54:26.950 | 01:12:49.300 | 00:31:07–00:33:12; ~35.21°S, 92.10°E | May be the same CL burst as the preferred candidate. This southern construction uses timing, not the preferred bearing; do not fuse incompatible source assignments. |
      | Nuisance/filter validation | None assigned | 01:03:13.150 | Unresolved | Publication trace cannot separate this from the periodic interference. Test signal preservation, not just suppression. |

      The raw-array batch should also cover the digitised early CL peaks **00:38:33.350 and 00:39:23.700** and the full Table 1 catalogue. The ~4-second offset between 00:38:29 and 00:38:33.350 could reflect pick conventions, but should not be resolved by assumption. The catalogue’s two entries at 00:52:36 with different bearings also need an author explanation. Retain all catalogue rows in the ledger, including low-compatibility ones.

      Milliseconds above are identifiers from stored digitised picks. They are **not millisecond accuracy in raw reception time**. The published line thickness alone prevents that interpretation. Older processing runs locate the late DG energy maximum around 01:12:49.5 rather than the deck’s 01:12:49.300: another reason to unify onset/peak definitions on the raw data before testing.

      ### What the existing diagnostic results do and do not say

      Our publication-trace controls do not establish a two-station association. The OSCAR boundary-aware test returned p = 0.632; the complete unfiltered correlation scan returned p = 1.000 and the control-selected filtered scan p = 0.964. The pulse-tail pairing time-slide control also returned p = 1.000. These are **different diagnostic tests** with different statistics, not probabilities that MH370 did or did not generate an event. The underlying plots were already filtered in the publication. They do not provide calibrated raw sensor noise or full three-channel bearing information. The source bundle documents the [diagnostic results and limitations](../../README.md) when viewed from the output directory; consult the original source README if opening this source file directly.

      ## Option A: minimum useful collaboration

      ### Three hypothesis families

      **Pléiades conditional.** If a stated subset of the imaged objects were aircraft debris and a specified drift family were valid, would a common impact produce a compatible CL/DG event? Keep BRAN, OSCAR and any model-average transport assumptions identifiable. Account for the fact that the initial events were selected by comparing them with a location model: agreement with the same PDF is not fresh, independent evidence. Use raw bearings estimated without a PDF-based steering restriction, held-out data and complete-search controls to assess what is genuinely new.

      **Unarrested high-rate descent conditional.** Use the explicitly selected Holland-bound descent cases with the altitude, velocity and timing assumptions needed to continue them to impact. Predict arrival intervals and received-signal ranges across coupling and propagation families. Ask which parts of that conditional would have been detectable, not whether all high-energy impacts must create an obvious plotted spike. Absence is not evidence against high-rate descent if plausible coupling/path/receiver models make the signal undetectable. Source kinetic energy is not equivalent to received pressure.

      **Catalogue and preferred-candidate conditionals.** Start with each named feature, estimate its direction independently, propagate timing uncertainty, and test consistency with the seventh arc and feasible post-final-transmission trajectories. Then examine the predicted DG direction/time region. Do not search only the region that makes a preferred event agree with the existing PDF.

      ### A small deliverable set

      1. **Author-verified ledger:** event definition, UTC, onset/peak uncertainty, passband, channel IDs, source-reported bearing and confidence, and any range assumption. Record duplicate features and the page 9 timing discrepancy explicitly.
      2. **One reproducible CL/DG pilot:** calibrated triads; direction/coherence plots; predicted versus observed time-frequency windows; unfiltered and filtered comparisons; matched negative controls and a modest blind-injection exercise. Fix the shortlist, allowed timing/angle search, filters and statistics before running the new test.
      3. **A short conclusions sheet:** for each hypothesis, compatible / inconsistent under stated assumptions / unresolved; what observation would change that judgement; whether a larger project is justified. An honest “not measurable with these data” is an acceptable result.

      Agree an effort ceiling with Kadri before requesting the work. A catalogue clarification and one small scripted batch may be the first commitment; it need not imply a promise to build a full beamformer immediately.

      ### Data and code arrangement

      Ideally use all three H01W and all three H08S hydrophone channels, verified sensor coordinates/depths, UTC timing corrections, response/calibration metadata, sample rates, gaps and original filtering settings. Ask for continuous target intervals and adequate pre/post and off-event control records. Preprocessing guards, travel-time uncertainties and detection calibration may require much more data than a cropped candidate window.

      If transfer is not authorised, supply a versioned script to run where the data are held. Return permitted research products: event and uncertainty tables, beam/coherence summaries, calibrated signal-level estimates, injection recovery curves, search-adjusted statistics, figures, configuration and hashes. Do not assume derived waveform files are automatically unrestricted. CTBTO vDEC gives organisation-based access under an approved project and contract; it prohibits third-party/raw-data redistribution while permitting publication of research outcomes. Confirm the actual agreement before sharing. [CTBTO vDEC, access and conditions of use](https://www.ctbto.org/resources/for-researchers-experts/vdec).

      Existing source-side raw-triad adapter/search code is a starting point, **not a validated beamforming or scientific likelihood implementation**. The first run should test units, clocks and array geometry with known/synthetic inputs. No new estimator weights should come from the existing airgun-confounded publication-trace correlations.

      ### DG suppression: proposed comparison, not a claimed best filter

      - **Spatial suppression:** infer interference direction and cross-channel covariance in held-out intervals; compare conventional and constrained/adaptive beamforming. Protect each tested target direction. Three sensors provide limited spatial degrees of freedom; a target close to the interference bearing or with similar multipath may not be separable.
      - **Cycle-aware subtraction:** fit changing shot times and a low-rank/template pulse family on other cycles; allow amplitude and waveform evolution. Avoid fitting away the candidate. Evaluate both residuals and unusually strong original pulses, with search correction.
      - **Joint nuisance modelling:** retain the data and model an airgun component plus a possible transient. Compare against an airgun-only model and blind injected targets. Prefer this to interpreting gaps introduced by masking as absence of a signal.

      AIS or, preferably, shot/navigation logs could constrain the interference model. If firing is distance-triggered, estimate shot epochs from travelled distance, a shot-spacing parameter and an unknown initial phase; then propagate from each shot position to each hydrophone. Fit clock offsets, missed shots and turns. Vessel position is not necessarily the towed source position. AIS is an optional timing prior, not a substitute for recorded shot times, and poor historical cadence may prevent useful phase prediction.

      ## Option B: physically informed evidence in the estimator

      The joint impact state must preserve correlations between location, time, velocity, mass and geometry. Later/farther glide trajectories may have lower impact energy on average, but this relationship must emerge from each terminal-flight model rather than be imposed as a universal rule. Carry source coupling, path/bathymetry, propagation family and receiver/interference response explicitly. A simple broad prior over coupling is a sensitivity analysis, not an empirically estimated distribution.

      Let `z_i` be an existing trajectory particle’s impact state; `theta` the uncertain acoustic parameters; `M` an explicit physical/statistical model family; and `y_CL,y_DG` the actual records or validated sufficient statistics. The proposed update is:

      ```latex
      w_i^{\mathrm{new}} \propto w_i
      \int p(y_{\mathrm{CL}},y_{\mathrm{DG}}\mid z_i,\theta,M)
            p(\theta\mid z_i,M)\,\mathrm{d}\theta .
      ```

      Include an unrelated-transient/interference explanation and calibrated selection effects. Use a joint model rather than multiplying correlated scores. For a specified detector and valid coverage, the target non-detection component is:

      ```latex
      P(\text{no target detection}\mid z_i,\theta,M)
       = 1-P_D(z_i,\theta,M).
      ```

      A full no-trigger likelihood also accounts for background false alarms. As an illustration only, no target detection produces factors 0.1 and 0.9 for otherwise equally weighted states with calibrated detection probabilities 0.9 and 0.1. That is a 9:1 relative preference for the latter **under that detector/model**, not a measured result here. With negligible detectability or unavailable coverage, absence should provide essentially no constraint.

      Validate source injections, noise mismatch, family sensitivity, timing uncertainty, false alarms, missed detections and the full search over positions, lags, frequencies and filters. Remote stations need not receive identical pressure waveforms: propagation can change dispersion and frequency content. Model their common source with station-specific responses. Treat acoustic-gravity and conventional acoustic approximations as explicit alternatives where appropriate, not a universal few-second timing correction.

      The illustrative one-year programme is: months 1–2 data QA and catalogue; 3–5 reduced-order source/propagation modelling; 6–8 detector and interference validation; 9–12 estimator integration and sensitivity reporting. This is a proposal, not an agreed schedule. A fully calibrated 777 water-entry model and basin-scale waveform model may exceed that scope. A useful minimum product is a reproducible, model-conditional likelihood with documented domains where it cannot constrain the search.

      The estimator should retain a predictive-only path and require a named, calibrated observed-signal model before likelihood evaluation. Compare with/without-acoustics posteriors using the same baseline evidence. Check effective sample size and support: reweighting cannot recover a region absent from the trajectory ensemble. Distinguish a finite-sample lack of support from physical impossibility.

      ## Funding and scope discussion

      The quoted £40–50k needs a Cardiff costing: it may describe salary rather than the full employment/research cost. At face value, a 25% contribution is £10–12.5k and leaves £30–37.5k. Ask for employer contributions, supervision, computing/data, publication, overhead and any VAT implications to be made explicit before committing.

      The first funding conversations should be Cardiff’s research office/IAA team and EPSRC Mathematical Sciences Small Grants. A forthcoming Lloyd’s Register Foundation small-grants call is a possible distinct pilot route, not a full postdoctoral salary solution. Exact eligibility and dates are in the private funding note; the slide summarises checked public information. A sponsor-facing one-page concept note can stress a transferable capability: **calibrated detection and non-detection of ocean-impact events in noisy hydroacoustic arrays**, with MH370 as a demanding case study. No detection or preferred search location should be promised.

      ## Publication compromise

      Your proposed compromise is sensible: develop the broad JON paper promptly, but do not make an acoustic conclusion a prerequisite for its release. Include only conditional results that the collaborators have checked and approved. If new raw-data tests are not ready, label that section as a public-data reproduction, limitations and proposed research; add the new results later. A subsequent methods/application paper should have genuinely new calibration and inference, not duplicate the first paper.

      JON explicitly permits preprints without treating them as prior/duplicate publication. Mark a preprint as not peer reviewed, follow the publisher’s version-linking policy, disclose relevant AI assistance, identify thesis-derived material, agree authorship/contributions, and obtain permission for shared results and figures. A preprint is not acceptance by JON. [JON publishing ethics](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/journal-of-navigation/information/journal-policies/publishing-ethics), [Cambridge preprint policy](https://www.cambridge.org/core/open-research/preprint-policy), [JON preparation instructions](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/journal-of-navigation/information/author-instructions/preparing-your-materials).

      There is a defensible operational reason to prepare early. The transport minister’s statement, reported on 29 June 2026, describes a contract extension to 30 June 2027 and an asset-rescheduling window from November 2026 to April 2027. That supports aiming to have a reviewed technical summary ready, but not promising a particular sailing date or pressuring collaborators to publish unfinished science. [Bernama, ministerial statement report](https://www.bernama.com/en/news.php?id=2574438).

      ## Suggested follow-up wording

      Dear Usama,

      Thank you again for yesterday’s discussion. I enjoyed it and am very pleased that you see possibilities for collaboration. I have attached the presentation and added a few slides suggesting how we might proceed, reflecting the two options we discussed.

      For Option A, I wondered whether we could agree a small, bounded set of checks: first clarifying the catalogue timings and bearings, then testing the preferred candidate and the 00:49:58 and 00:53:31 entries, with a limited two-station exercise where useful. I have also included the ~00:52 feature as a bearing check, and the early-impact and Pléiades conditionals. The aim would be to establish what can and cannot be concluded without asking you to commit substantial time at this stage.

      For Option B, I would be interested in helping develop a costed postdoctoral project to calibrate how hydroacoustic detections and non-detections could enter the Bayesian estimator. I could potentially contribute around a quarter of the £40–50k figure we discussed, subject to confirming the full cost, and help look for other support. Would you be comfortable introducing us to the relevant Cardiff research-funding or partnerships colleague?

      On publication, my thought is to progress the broader JON paper while keeping any shared hydroacoustic results limited to what we have had time to check and agree. That could point clearly towards the larger programme, without putting a deadline on the deeper work. I would welcome your preference on scope and pace, and which initial checks would be most useful and practical.

      Thanks again,

      Pete

      # Funding and giving: private planning note for Pete

      Checked **12 September 2026**. Separate from the shareable scientific slides. This is a screening of routes to discuss with Cardiff, not a funding commitment or personalised tax advice. Residence, US citizenship/tax status, business jurisdiction and the donation year materially affect the answer.

      ## My recommendation

      Start with **a Cardiff-hosted research project**, not a new nonprofit. Ask Kadri to introduce a research-services/finance colleague and the university’s partnerships or development team. Obtain a full costing, choose a genuine research-sponsorship or philanthropic route, and then approach two or three suitable funders. A formal business partnership can help when there is a real business research objective; it is not a way to turn any personal research interest into a matched grant.

      ### Budget arithmetic — not yet a university quote

      | Interpretation | Total | Your 25% | Balance |
      | --- | --- | --- | --- |
      | Lower discussion figure | £40,000 | £10,000 | £30,000 |
      | Upper discussion figure | £50,000 | £12,500 | £37,500 |

      First ask whether the figure includes employer National Insurance/pension, grade and salary increments, PI supervision, computing/data, travel, open-access costs, estates/indirect costs and any contractual VAT treatment. A salary-only £40–50k is not automatically the price of a one-year project. Do not assume a grant’s full-economic-cost contribution maps directly onto the cash salary gap.

      ## Prioritised funding routes

      ### 1. Cardiff research partnerships and Impact Acceleration Accounts — first enquiry

      Cardiff operates IAAs to develop the impact of research and work with external partners. Ask whether Kadri’s existing research supports an eligible pilot under a current internal call; whether staff time, feasibility work, business/charitable partners or external cash/in-kind contributions are eligible; and whether the school can contribute time or infrastructure. **I found no public, automatic “donate 25%, Cardiff supplies 75%” scheme or guaranteed match ratio.**

      Contact **<email>**; the published operations contacts include Phil Lambert, Impact Funding Officer, **<email>**, and Dr Harriet Mills, Impact Funding Manager, **<email>**. Prefer an introduction through Kadri. [Cardiff IAA programme and contacts](https://www.cardiff.ac.uk/research/impact-and-innovation/impact-acceleration-accounts).

      Suggested question: “Could a jointly funded feasibility study based on Dr Kadri’s hydroacoustics research qualify for an internal IAA or partnership award, and what existing-research linkage, partner contribution and staff-cost rules apply?”

      ### 2. EPSRC Mathematical Sciences Small Grants — strongest named grant to assess

      This rolling scheme normally supports **up to £100,000 full economic cost**, with **80% funded by EPSRC**, for **up to 12 months**. It supports original research, new collaborations and postdoctoral development; most work must be within mathematical sciences. Cardiff would submit, not you personally. Applications can be made without a published deadline, but that does not guarantee a rapid decision. Unnamed postdoctoral appointments shorter than six months are discouraged.

      The inverse-problem/Bayesian detection component appears a plausible fit **in my judgement**, subject to Kadri and EPSRC confirming the remit. The standard 20% FEC contribution is not a donor-matching offer; additional institutional match must not influence assessment, while third-party contributions are encouraged. Ask how your proposed support should be accounted for without double funding. Enquiry: **<email>**. [EPSRC scheme, scope, duration, funding and matched-funding rules](https://www.ukri.org/opportunity/mathematical-sciences-small-grants/).

      ### 3. A larger EPSRC responsive-mode application — if the scope grows

      EPSRC’s standard research grant route covers engineering and physical sciences, including mathematical sciences; the current opportunity has no fixed maximum duration/value and normally funds 80% FEC. This is worth considering for a broader detection/inverse-modelling programme, but not assuming that a £40–50k salary request alone constitutes a competitive research proposal. It needs a clear research advance, methods and validation. [EPSRC standard responsive-mode grant](https://www.ukri.org/opportunity/epsrc-standard-research-grant-nov-2023-responsive-mode/).

      ### 4. Lloyd’s Register Foundation — a modest pilot possibility

      The live small-grants page states that the next application window is **19 October–14 December 2026**, with grants **up to £10,000** for projects **up to 12 months**. It permits non-duplicative co-funding and proof-of-concept work aligned with its engineering-safety/public-benefit mission. It **does not fund full salaries or general overheads**; proportionate project staff time can be eligible. This is a possible separate pilot or validation activity, not a full-year postdoc salary fund.

      Frame any enquiry around transferable ocean-event detection/safety methods, with a genuine public-benefit output, not simply locating one aircraft. Fit is unconfirmed. The general calls page currently lists several larger programmes under **closed** calls, so I would not present those as live options. [Small grants programme, including 2026 dates and FAQs 5–8](https://www.lrfoundation.org.uk/news/small-grants-scheme), [current calls list](https://www.lrfoundation.org.uk/calls-for-funding).

      ### 5. Knowledge Transfer Partnerships — only for a real business project

      KTP is a genuine university–business route for embedding academic knowledge in a business innovation project. Published 2026–27 Round 1 rules allow contributions up to **67% for SMEs** or **50% for large companies**; the partner pays the remaining eligible costs and other required costs. **That round closed on 1 April 2026**; use it as verified programme guidance, not an invitation to apply to a live call. Cardiff should identify the current round and adviser.

      A passive sponsor or newly formed shell nonprofit is not enough. Ask whether an actual business intends to develop and use a hydroacoustic/inverse-analysis capability, can meet eligibility and management requirements, and can fund the real contribution. At your proposed £10–12.5k, do not assume a year-long KTP is affordable. [Innovate UK official KTP competition rules](https://apply-for-innovation-funding.service.gov.uk/competition/2380/overview/3e170dd3-4458-4c7e-a096-e0ea50b88040), [Cardiff business-partnership entry point](https://www.cardiff.ac.uk/work-with-us).

      ### Sponsors and aviation/MH370-related organisations

      I did not verify a currently open, dedicated MH370 research grant that would reliably cover this post. That is a search limitation, not proof that no support exists. My suggested outreach order is:

      - **One or two private co-sponsors through Cardiff’s development team.** Conditional on the full quote really being £40–50k, three additional equal contributors at £10–12.5k would close the nominal gap. A named research fund could consolidate gifts without a new entity.
      - **Flight Safety Foundation as a research partner/introduction route.** Its published mission includes impartial research and collaboration; I did not find a relevant open postdoc-grant scheme on the pages reviewed. Ask for technical review, aviation-safety contacts or a sponsor introduction rather than assuming it is a grant-maker. [Foundation mission](https://flightsafety.org/foundation/).
      - **Potential industry supporters:** Ocean Infinity/search technology, satellite-communications suppliers, marine geophysics/hydrophone companies, aircraft/engine manufacturers and aviation insurers. These are proposed prospects, not verified interested sponsors. A specific in-kind request—calibration, shot logs, engineering advice, computing—may be more realistic initially than cash.
      - **MH370-family and search-community contacts:** seek advice on the research priorities and introductions, without assuming families should fund it. Avoid marketing the work as a likely discovery or implying sponsor endorsement of a location.

      Use a short concept note, a properly costed budget and a deliverable list. Preserve freedom to publish negative findings, document conflicts of interest, and avoid sponsor control over conclusions. No approaches have been sent.

      ## Does forming a nonprofit help?

      For this single university project, **probably not enough to justify starting there**. It could become useful if you intend a continuing, multi-project research programme, need an independent governance structure, or will raise substantial contributions from many donors. It does not itself create matched funding or automatic tax deductibility in both countries.

      England/Wales charity formation involves trustees, a governing document, charitable purposes and demonstrable public benefit; HMRC recognition is needed for charity tax relief. Administration and reporting are real obligations. A structure intended mainly to benefit a founder’s business or confer private ownership of funded research needs specialist scrutiny. The Charity Commission itself points applicants towards alternatives to forming a charity. [Government charity-formation guidance](https://www.gov.uk/setting-up-charity).

      A restricted university gift, an approved existing grant-making charity or a suitable donor-advised/fiscal-sponsorship arrangement is simpler to investigate first. Research donations and commercial research contracts are different: ask Cardiff to document which you are actually arranging, including deliverables, IP, publication rights and benefits.

      ## UK/US tax: useful routes, conditional on your circumstances

      ### UK personal giving

      A qualifying gift with a valid Gift Aid declaration can add 25% to the amount received by the charity if sufficient UK income/capital-gains tax has been paid. Higher-rate relief may also be available. Illustratively, a qualifying **£10,000 personal gift becomes £12,500 for the charity**; at a 40% marginal rate, additional relief can be £2,500, giving a £7,500 net cost. That is tax relief, not university matching. It is not a calculation of your entitlement. [HMRC Gift Aid guidance and worked example](https://www.gov.uk/donating-to-charity/gift-aid).

      ### UK company contribution

      A qualifying company donation can generally be deducted from business profits for corporation-tax purposes. This is **not personal Gift Aid**, and a company donation is not grossed up by the charity in the same way. If the company receives advertising or another related benefit, sponsorship rules may apply; if it is commissioning research/IP, Cardiff and the adviser should assess it as the appropriate contract rather than calling it a gift. Do not double claim personal and corporate relief on the same payment. [Company donations](https://www.gov.uk/tax-limited-company-gives-to-charity/donating-money), [company sponsorship](https://www.gov.uk/tax-limited-company-gives-to-charity/sponsoring-a-charity), [HMRC detailed Gift Aid guidance, §3.11–3.12](https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/charities-detailed-guidance-notes/chapter-3-gift-aid).

      ### US personal giving: an existing Cardiff route

      Cardiff explicitly directs US donors to the **British Schools and Universities Foundation (BSUF)**. BSUF identifies itself as a domestic **501(c)(3)** charity, and Cardiff University appears in its approved-institutions list. This may allow an eligible US charitable deduction, subject to donor circumstances and the year’s rules. Cardiff’s webpage contains a “503(c)(3)” typo; BSUF’s own statement gives 501(c)(3). [Cardiff’s US giving instructions](https://www.cardiff.ac.uk/donate/other-ways-to-give), [BSUF purposes and policies](https://www.bsuf.org/about-bsuf), [approved institutions](https://www.bsuf.org/institutions).

      This must be a real donation through a charity exercising discretion and control—not a binding pass-through to a foreign recipient. US deductions generally do not apply to direct gifts to foreign organisations outside specified exceptions. Ask BSUF/Cardiff to approve the project and restrictions before sending funds. BSUF publishes quarterly board meetings: as of this note, the next listed meeting is **8 December 2026**, so confirm grant-release timing if the pilot needs immediate cash. [IRS Publication 526, nonqualified/foreign organisations](https://www.irs.gov/publications/p526), [BSUF board schedule and discretion](https://www.bsuf.org/about-bsuf).

      Do not use a 2025 tax-table assumption to calculate a 2026 donation’s net cost. Ask a US adviser to apply the rules for your donation year, itemisation/income limits, asset type, entity and any state relief. No US net-cost percentage is assumed here.

      ### Dual UK/US taxpayer

      Investigate the **CAF American Donor Fund**, a dual-qualified giving service designed for UK/US taxpayers. Its current page also offers a one-off Donor Advised Gift service with no minimum contribution. It can provide an existing cross-border structure without your forming two entities. Confirm fees, grant timing, Cardiff/project eligibility and the interaction of UK relief, US deductions and foreign-tax credits with your adviser. Do not simply add UK and US marginal relief percentages. [CAF dual-taxpayer giving](https://www.cafonline.org/personal-giving/effective-ways-to-give/dual-tax-payer-donations).

      **Practical next step:** decide whether funds come from you or a business, then ask Cardiff development and your UK/US adviser to compare a direct restricted gift, BSUF, CAF and a research contract. Only then consider a new nonprofit. Your residence/citizenship, company jurisdiction and intended economic benefits are still unknown here.

      ## Suggested questions to Cardiff

      1. What is the full cost of a six-month and a twelve-month researcher appointment, and what exactly did the £40–50k estimate include?
      2. Could a limited pilot qualify under an IAA/internal partnership call, and is there any discretionary school contribution or philanthropic match currently available?
      3. Does EPSRC Mathematical Sciences Small Grants fit the research advance, and who will lead the remit enquiry/application?
      4. How can partner cash/in-kind support sit alongside the proposed grant without double funding or compromising publication independence?
      5. Can development establish an agreed restricted project fund and receive UK or US-qualified gifts through existing arrangements?
      6. What project/IP/publication/data terms apply to a business sponsor compared with a donor? How quickly can a researcher actually start?

### Scientific next steps

I suggest starting with CL **00:54:30, 00:49:58, 00:53:31 and the ~00:52 feature**, followed by targeted DG checks. Retain **00:39:02** as an early-impact control.

The “00:38 and 00:55” slide actually shows the catalogue spanning **00:38:29–00:55:07**, not two additional detections. The notes also flag the paper’s inconsistent description of the preferred candidate’s timing. [Kadri’s paper](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-024-60529-1).

Your proposed Option B interpretation is right: calibrated acoustic evidence could redistribute trajectory weights. The crucial qualification is that **absence can penalise a trajectory only if its signal should have been detectable under the stated assumptions**.

### Funding

My first move would be a Cardiff introduction and full costing. The £40–50k may exclude employer costs and overheads.

**EPSRC Mathematical Sciences Small Grants** looks particularly worth discussing with Kadri; it supports projects up to twelve months, subject to mathematical-sciences remit and competitive assessment. It is not an automatic donor-match scheme. [Scheme details](https://www.ukri.org/opportunity/mathematical-sciences-small-grants/).

I would **not form a new nonprofit yet**. Cardiff already offers US giving through BSUF; the separate note compares this with UK gifts, business sponsorship and dual-qualified giving. Your actual tax treatment needs your adviser’s assessment. [Cardiff’s giving arrangements](https://www.cardiff.ac.uk/donate/other-ways-to-give).

### Publication

I support your compromise: progress the broader JON paper/preprint, include only checked and jointly approved acoustic results, and leave the deeper programme on an agreed research timetable. JON explicitly permits preprints. [Journal policy](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/journal-of-navigation/information/journal-policies/publishing-ethics).

      11 filter  Hz band. Noting that the broadband fre-
      quency content is typical for the propagation of acoustic-gravity ­waves15 that can travel far distances from the 
      event location, a high pass Butterworth IIR filter was used (in addition to the CTBTO high pass filter) below 5 Hz. 
      Moreover, a 2–40 [Hz] band-pass filter was applied in general. Filtering noise, enhancing the signal appearance 
      and stabilising the time-waveform can be crucial for identifying the bearing of the signal of interest, in particular 
      when the source is an impulse that generates short signals of a few seconds length.
      Each hydrophone station has three hydrophones configured in a triangular shape spaced by around 2 km. 
      Exact locations of all hydrophones can be obtained from the CTBTO. In information theory, entropy serves as 
      a measure quantifying the amount of information encapsulated within a signal. Let P(x) denote the probability 
      distribution function of the discrete-

      in earthquake seem to be between 2.5 and 6 Hz filtering allows calculating a bearing of 63.7◦ with a relatively 
      large deviation of 3.3◦ (see Fig. 17), which is about 140 km off the epicentre.
      Figure 14.   Distance (top) and time (bottom) that should have been travelled by MH370 after the last 
      handshake (7th arc), as function of the average velocity (defined positive in the bearing direction.).
